# MC Roadmap

<!-- Dual identity:
     - In canonical MC (this repo), ROADMAP.md is the framework backlog.
     - In downstream consumer projects, ROADMAP.md is the consumer's product
       roadmap. Consumers receive a clean scaffold from
       `scripts/mc/generate-roadmap-scaffold.js` (which encodes the
       scaffold as an inline JS string, independent of this file).
     - Sync is ONE-WAY (canonical → product) via /mc:setup + /mc:update.
       Products never push back to canonical. The consumer's ROADMAP.md is
       owned by the consumer; canonical ROADMAP.md is never propagated. -->

## Strategy

MC exists to help the maintainer ship real products faster while extracting reusable open-source automation as a byproduct. The near-term roadmap prioritizes **trust and distribution integrity**: never leak product data, never ship broken installs, and reduce friction across portfolio repos. Work that does not unblock product shipping or public framework reliability stays parked.

**Architectural framing (2026-05-22).** MC is a **managed configuration layer over the host project's `.claude/` interface**, not a content blob. The framework's source-of-truth lives at `_mc/` in every installed product; `.claude/` is the *compiled runtime interface* Claude Code consumes. Ownership is declared by `_mc/MANIFEST.json`, not by path prefix. Sync is **one-way**: canonical MC → products via `/mc:setup` + `/mc:update`. Products never push back **code** — there is no automated upstream code-promote channel (no `/warp:promote`, no `mc-to-update.md` ledger; that surface stays retired, SP-20260522-001). Discoveries flow into canonical through the **maintainer's own judgment**, now *structured* by the `mc:flag` + `mc:reconcile` tooling (2026-05-26): downstream `/mc:flag` writes a human-readable `MC.md` gap register; canonical `/mc:reconcile` discovers every product's register, **verifies each gap @current** (≈half are usually already fixed — MC ED-008), and the maintainer acts in canonical. These **formalize** "read the products you maintain, act in canonical directly" — they do NOT auto-push code. *(`/mc:flag` is redefined here as the gap-register producer, distinct from the purged promote-flag; the no-automated-promote stance from the 2026-05-22 codex consults is unchanged.)*

**Cadence rule.** After two consecutive MC infrastructure sprints, the next sprint must ship product value in a real portfolio product (Jobzooka, DreamTeam, future). Any framework work must name the product blocker it removes; framework work without a named product blocker goes to Later. *(Origin: 2026-05-21 codex product-lead review — 8 framework sprints / 0 product-delivery sprints in the prior 10 days was the warning sign that prompted the rule.)*

**Reading the backlog.** Sections are ordered by urgency, not by phase: **Now** = current sprint window (boundary + identity and install integrity are the trust-blockers; everything else waits); **Next** = ready when Now empties; **Later** = parked with named conditions for revival; **Archive Index** = research notes + postmortems that informed decisions but are not active backlog. The legacy `Phase 1/2/3/4` structure was retired 2026-05-21 — historical items either shipped (preserved in version-history sections) or were absorbed into Now / Next / Later.

**Lifecycle tags** on backlog entries: `[open]`, `[in-progress]`, `[fixed-local]`, `[shipped]`, `[duplicate]`, `[blocked]`, `[deferred]`, `[parked]`. *(2026-05-22: `[promote-ready]` and `[promoted]` retired alongside `/warp:promote` deletion — fixes now move directly from `[fixed-local]` to `[shipped]` via canonical release, not via product→canonical promote.)*

Canonical examples: `/cockpit:readiness` (`fa36772f`), `/portfolio:run`; delivery vehicle = `/mc:update`; correctness gate = the executable consumer-contract gate (#1). **Key seam (SG-A↔SG-B):** the run-in-product boundary and the consumer-contract keystone are the same problem from two ends — does the framework reliably deliver a capability into a product's own session?

#### E-SYSTEM-ORG-001 — Agent-System Organization & Canonical Source-of-Truth Cleanup (`.system` files/folders) — ⭐⭐ TOP (operator-directed 2026-06-07)
- **Goal:** ONE canonical source of truth per agent-system artifact — no duplicate, drifted, orphaned, or mispointed `.system` files/folders. The ADR-0007 org-rewrite left a CLASS of these (a copy made into the new `.claude/agents/` tree while the maintained canonical stayed at the old `.claude/project/reference/` location, pointers aimed at one while the operator/agents look at the other), which is the structural root behind "why do agents keep skipping/misreading the dispatch guide" and similar recurring confusion.
- **Priority:** ⭐⭐ TOP (operator-directed 2026-06-07 — "add figuring all this out to the top of the roadmap; pull in the tasks on fixing the organization and all the 'system' files and folders"). **State:** Active — in-canonical work COMPLETE; the open ~1% is two operator-gated/bounded items only (§13.7 instrumented A/B measurement + S-5 operator-scoped repo move). **Completion:** ~99% (reconciled 2026-06-27 — the ROADMAP summary was stale "Planned 0%" while the epic tracker had been driven to ~99% Active; see the epic file Change log 2026-06-08).
- **Epic tracker:** [trackers/epics/E-SYSTEM-ORG-001-agent-system-org-cleanup.md](trackers/epics/E-SYSTEM-ORG-001-agent-system-org-cleanup.md).
- **Proven instance (the forcing example, diagnosed 2026-06-07):** `agent-dispatch-guide.md` exists TWICE and has drifted — `.claude/project/reference/…` (197 ln, edited 2026-06-05, post-ADR-0007 roles; the one `paths.agentDispatchGuide` + the session-start banner + CLAUDE.md correctly point to) AND `.claude/agents/.system/guides/…` (243 ln, edited 2026-06-02, pre-ADR-0007, orphaned from the pointer but sitting in the intuitive agent-tree home). Both `owner=framework` → **both ship downstream**. The stale copy squats in the intuitive location, so operator + agents keep pointing at different files. `scan:references` can't catch it (both files exist = not a broken ref; it's content drift).
- **Scope / pulled-in tasks (consolidated here per operator 2026-06-07):** (a) **E-DISPATCH-INTEGRITY-001 F-4/F-5** — agents-folder de-dot + file-org (same work as ED-026 — route once, execute once here); (b) **ED-026 cutover-completeness** — `scan:cutover-completeness` already greps raw deleted-old-tree literals; extend it to duplicate/drift; (c) **"Maintainer canonical scrub orchestration"** (Sprint-10 candidate — move MC-as-product specs out of canonical root, flip `ROOT_LEAK_PENDING_SCRUB`); (d) **E-BOUNDARY-001** framework-boundary closure (the repo-move the framework can't self-execute).

#### E-DISPATCH-INTEGRITY-001 — Agent Dispatch & Orchestration Integrity (+ agents-folder de-dot) — ⭐ TOP (bundles into MC-READINESS)
- **Goal:** End the recurring "dispatch done wrong / sprint theater" class — modes spawn teams that are never used; `/sprint:full` emits *coverage* telemetry with zero real dispatch yet reads green; reaped lanes leave no record. Make a real, recorded (`ok:true`) dispatch the ONLY way a phase counts as covered; collapse the divergent dispatch entrypoints toward one; and de-dot/restructure the confusing agents folder.
- **Priority:** ⭐ TOP (operator-directed 2026-06-07 — the root behind every "where's the team?" + faked-conduct incident this org keeps hitting). **State:** Active. **Completion:** 0% (diagnosed cross-provider; fixes scoped).
- **Epic tracker:** [trackers/epics/E-DISPATCH-INTEGRITY-001-agent-dispatch-integrity.md](trackers/epics/E-DISPATCH-INTEGRITY-001-agent-dispatch-integrity.md).
- **Cross-provider diagnosis (2026-06-07):** Claude (deep crawl + torture-test, full tool access) + GPT-5.5 (tight, inlined) dispatched as independent diagnosticians — *correctly*, via bash subprocess (`claude -p --agent` + `dispatch-agent.js`). **Convergence:** the dispatch *primitives* (route-guard 12/12, `dispatch-claude.js` reap-catcher, `gauntlet-verify`, the cross-provider bridge — real `qa`→openai PONG in 5.9s) are individually sound + torture-test green; the ORCHESTRATION layer above them is broken. **Key divergence (the value of cross-provider):** GPT judged the hidden-`.` dirs a LIKELY ROOT CAUSE; Claude tested it 4 ways (0 evidence in 24,433 events; manifest walker + spec-resolver + harness Glob/Grep all traverse dot-dirs) and ruled it **NOT** the cause — a real maintainability hazard, not the dispatch failure. Evidence: `runtime/diagnostic/{log-mining,contract-map}.md` + `tt/` + `.claude/runtime/diagnostics/diag-{claude,gpt2}.json`. (A 3rd live data point: the GPT deep pass itself TIMED OUT at the 900s bridge bound — the cross-provider route can't survive a long task.)
- **Root causes (ranked, grounded):** **RC-2 (deepest)** telemetry-only by default — `/sprint:full` → `full.js` with no `--epsilon-dispatch` writes `manager_consult` *coverage* records with zero real dispatch that `scan:sprint-manager-consult`/`scan:sprint-hook-coverage` read green = theater in the default path. **RC-1** the two-world execution seam — a node subprocess (`full.js`/`epsilon-runtime.js`) structurally CANNOT reach the in-process team/Agent tool (`full.js:653`, `epsilon-runtime.js:442-444`) → spawned-but-orphaned team. **RC-3** the reap (RI-004/ED-018) → ε stopped dispatching builders because raw `claude -p --agent` reaps with no record. **RC-4** `gauntlet-verify` sound but caller-dependent (no embedded runId; a historic `ok:true` greens a never-run lane — demonstrated live). **RC-5** hidden-`.` = NOT the cause (operator hypothesis tested + refuted).
- **Related sprints (planned):** F-1 false-green-kill (a phase is "covered" only with a backing `ok:true` record) · F-2 two-world-seam resolution (World-A conductor mandatory; demote `full.js` to a ledger/state helper that never emits dispatch-telemetry it didn't perform) · F-3 `gauntlet-verify` runId/`--since` correlation · F-4 agents-folder de-dot + one canonical dispatch-guide (delete the stale-and-wrong orphan) · F-5 silent-surface hardening (fallback writes a record; reap-census; `cli.js` skip-by-visible-`_` not dot).
- **Dependencies:** none hard (pairs with the parked "simplify .claude/agents" item, now absorbed as F-4). **Rationale:** the apparatus is fine; the orchestration above it lets *coverage* exist with no real dispatch behind it. **Expected impact:** "sprint theater" becomes structurally impossible — no phase can be "covered" without a real, recorded dispatch; one dispatch path; a sane, visible agents tree.
- **Current next action:** F-1 — make a backing `ok:true` completion record the precondition for phase coverage (`full.js:95-116` + the two coverage enforcers); either default `/sprint:full` to `--epsilon-dispatch` or reject the non-dispatch coverage path.
- **Related definitions:** Wiring, Verification, Evidence, Dispatch, Completion.

- **Priority:** Medium (deferred pre-mvp; pull forward when a 2nd external consumer needs differentiated risk). **State:** Planned. **Completion:** 0%.
- **Epic tracker:** [trackers/epics/E-STABLE-CHANNEL-001-stable-lts-channel.md](trackers/epics/E-STABLE-CHANNEL-001-stable-lts-channel.md).

- **Dependencies:** E-TEST-SUITE-001's regression seed (defines "stable"); E-GOLDEN-FLOW-001's contract gate (the promotion-boundary substance). **Rationale:** downstream tracks a single rolling latest and inherits hollow-rung/regressed releases. **Expected impact:** the operator (and external users) can choose stability over freshness.
- **Current next action:** none yet (Planned; activate after E-TEST-SUITE-001 + E-GOLDEN-FLOW-001 land). **Related definitions:** Roadmap, Epic, Evidence. **Detail:** `🟡 0.18.0` block below.

#### E-LEAN-GAUNTLET-001 — Development-Process Speed: touchpoint-based + risk-based testing replaces the whole-app gauntlet — ⭐ HIGH (operator-directed 2026-09-18, DISCUSSED-2026-09-18)
- **Goal:** the time from "change built" to "change landed" drops by an order of magnitude because the system tests **what changed and what it touches**, at a depth set by **declared risk**, instead of re-testing the entire framework on every edit. Source: the operator's DUBS QA process (`DUBS-QA-Process-GPT.docx`, supplied three times; final instruction 2026-09-18), transferred selectively — DUBS was a human QA team on a consumer app; MC is a 100%-AI team whose product IS the development system, so only the structural ideas carry, never the phases or the human rituals.
- **Measured slow points (this is what the epic removes, ranked by cost):**
  1. **Whole-app gauntlet per change.** Every fix cycle re-ran the full reviewer set (backend + qa + security, each cross-provider) on the whole surface. S-OS-06 needed 4 rounds and 14 days for an identifier rename whose content-grain oracle was 0 violations from round 2 on.
  2. **Proof-of-proof recursion.** r4 produced 122 α rulings and 523 β ledger rows to certify a zero: enforcers got falsifiers, falsifiers got vacuity counts, vacuity counts got hermeticity scrubs, scrubs got platform floors. Each step was individually correct and collectively unbounded — there was no rule that says when a measured zero is *enough*.
  3. **β consult at every boundary, serialized.** Consults are landing preconditions by construction (correct), but they were issued one at a time and each one could open a new lane.
  4. **Single-platform local + CI round trips.** Every local zero certifies win32 only; the second platform (linux) is only reachable through an operator push, so each platform-shaped finding costs a human round trip.
  5. **Manifest/register regen churn.** ROADMAP.md, `scripts/**` and `.claude/commands/**` are hash-tracked; touching any of them forces a triple manifest regen + occurrence-register regen "last before commit", and a stale regen is a RED gate (BC-02/BC-05) — the regen itself became a recurring source of REDs.
  6. **Stub-first / report-to-file / reap-survival protocols on every dispatch** — necessary against RI-004, but paid on tiny lanes too.
- **What transfers from DUBS (and how it maps to MC):**
  - **Glossary first.** A committed `_planning/GOLDEN-PATHS.md` naming MC's **Golden Features** (install/update via `/mc:*`, the sprint lifecycle `/sprint:full`, dispatch `dispatch-claude.js`/`dispatch-agent.js`, the leak gate, the tracker validator) and their **Golden Paths**; everything else is **Silver**. The DUBS rule "all tests derive from the glossary" becomes "no test package without a path classification."
  - **Change classification drives the package.** DUBS: Feature Release / Feature Update / Bugfix + the dev team declares *major vs minor* and the *risk areas*. MC: the spec / ticket / commit trailer carries `change: bugfix|update|release`, `blast: minor|major`, and `risks:` (the DUBS "major risks for QA to test" list) — the gauntlet reads these, it does not guess.
  - **Focused vs Touchpoint.** DUBS: *Focused = what we intentionally changed*; *Variable Touchpoint = what we did not intend to change but might have been affected*. MC computes both mechanically: Focused = the diff's files + their tests; Touchpoint = the reverse-dependency closure (require graph + `paths.registry` consumers + hook wiring + skill cross-refs, i.e. the maps under `paths.maps/`), positive-path depth only. Everything outside both sets is NOT tested on that change.
  - **Scope × Depth taxonomy.** DUBS test types are (Scope: Focused/Broad/All/Variable) × (Depth: Shallow/Medium/Deep). MC's package per change: **Sanity** (Focused/Shallow — did the change land, does the test that names it pass), **Combined Focused** (Focused/Deep — positive + negative for the changed unit), **Touchpoint** (Variable/Medium — positive paths of adjacent units), **Smoke** (Broad/Shallow — Golden Paths only, always, cheap). **Full E2E (All/Deep)** runs only at a release candidate or every 4th sprint — exactly DUBS's quarterly cadence — never per change.
  - **Order + catastrophic-fail rule.** Tests run in order; a catastrophic fail in an earlier stage stops later stages (no point cross-provider-reviewing a build whose sanity failed). After a fix, DUBS's rule verbatim: *a targeted re-test is sufficient and preferred; a full re-execution is required only when the fix has broad or unbounded impact, introduces many regressions, or indicates systemic instability.* Reviewer re-runs are scoped to the fix brief, not the sprint.
  - **Risk-based reviewer routing.** Security review runs Deep only when `risks:` or the touched paths intersect the security surface (auth, secrets, dispatch, leak gate, CI); otherwise it is a Shallow smoke. Cross-provider (second-model) review is reserved for `blast: major` — a `minor` bugfix gets one Claude reviewer on the Focused set.
  - **Time-box as a gate input.** DUBS time-boxed staging confirmation to 25–40% of the build's QA effort. MC: each package declares a budget (wall-clock + dispatches); exceeding it is a *finding about the process*, surfaced to the operator, not silently absorbed by another round.
  - **"Enough" is a rule, not a feeling.** New stop rule for the proof recursion: a gate is DISCHARGED when its declared oracle is measured zero on the certifying head **once**, on the platforms the release declares; further falsifiers/vacuity probes are their own roadmap items, never added mid-round. (Retro source: S-OS-06 r4.)
- **What does NOT transfer:** Dev/QA/Prod environment promotion phases, App Store tests, network/carrier tests, the human Vibe Check / Build Review / Golden-User assessments, Phase Four human approval of release candidates (the operator's push-to-main IS that step already), Phase Five one-week live checks.
- **Priority:** ⭐ HIGH — operator 2026-09-18: "the gauntlet is taking an immense amount of time… We need to not be testing the entire app every time we make a change." **State:** Planned (DISCUSSED-2026-09-18; NOT started inside S-OS-06 — the operator authorized fixing it before the rebrand closes but the final head must not grow a new surface). **Completion:** 0%.
- **Epic tracker:** not yet minted — mint with `/epic:plan` as the first action after E-OPEN-SOURCE-001 closes; the plan artifact should split into (1) glossary + change-classification trailer + enforcer, (2) touchpoint computation from the maps, (3) gauntlet package selector in `scripts/sprint/` reading the trailer, (4) the "enough" stop rule wired into the β boundary protocol + `/sprint:release`.
- **Dependencies:** E-OPEN-SOURCE-001 closed (final rebrand head landed); `paths.maps/` reverse-dependency graphs current (`/maps:all`). **Rationale:** two weeks for a rename is the cost of Scope=All/Depth=Deep on every change. **Expected impact:** a minor bugfix lands in one dispatch + one Focused review; a sprint's gauntlet is bounded by its declared blast radius, not by the size of the repo.
- **Enforcer (named at write-time per CLAUDE.md):** a gauntlet run without a change classification trailer refuses (fail-closed); the package selector logs `gauntlet-package-selected` with scope/depth/budget to `paths.eventsFile` so drift back to Scope=All is observable. Until built: log as enforcement debt at epic mint.
- **Related definitions:** Gauntlet, Sprint, Epic, Enforcer.

#### E-SELF-MONITOR-001 — Self-Monitoring Loop: the system measures its own process, detects slow points + recurring blockers, and acts on them; operator-facing messages get a fixed visual contract — ⭐ HIGH (operator-directed 2026-09-18, DISCUSSED-2026-09-18)
- **Goal:** the company watches ITSELF the way it watches a product — every session produces process telemetry, a detector turns it into findings ("this step took 10× its budget", "this blocker has now recurred 4 times"), and findings become decisions (a fix, a roadmap item, or a rule change) WITHOUT the operator having to notice the problem first. Operator's words (2026-09-18): "a mechanism or system that allows you to monitor yourself and make decisions based on that."
- **Three operator-named capabilities:**
  1. **Time sinks get detected and fixed.** Every dispatch, gate, consult, commit hook and wait is already timestamped (`paths.eventsFile`, `dispatch-completions.jsonl`, `paths.betaEvents`, git). Add declared BUDGETS per step class (dispatch, β consult round-trip, pre-commit hooks, gauntlet lane, regen) and a detector that flags budget overruns and ranks where the wall-clock actually went per sprint. Seed evidence from S-OS-06: pre-commit hooks 2–4 min per commit (exceeds the teammate foreground cap, so every commit is backgrounded + polled); β consult → ledger row latency measured in HOURS; gemini lanes ~6 min each ×5 ×2 rounds; 14 days for a rename.
  2. **Recurring blockers get detected and fixed.** A blocker taxonomy with a recurrence counter, fed automatically (not by memory): message-routing failures (teammate→lead messages batch until the lead's turn yields — the lead was unreachable ~2.5 h on 2026-09-18 while ε sent ~25 messages; sub-agent hand-backs route to the top-level instead of the spawning teammate, so ε recorded the binding security lane as NO-RECORD while its verdict sat with α; β verdicts reaching consumers as messages but not the ledger — third recurrence), harness classifier denials (push-to-main, workflow edits, turbo re-grant — the root cause of the operator being handed `!` commands), piped-exit-code traps (bit α and ε again 2026-09-18), stale-cwd / forked-ledger relocations. Rule: a blocker class at recurrence ≥3 auto-files a roadmap/ED item with its measured cost and a proposed structural fix; `/issues:log` + `paths.recurringIssues` are the existing store — the gap is that nothing WRITES to it automatically or READS it at session start.
  3. **Operator-facing message contract.** A fixed, visually distinct styling for anything the operator must SEE or ACT on, so it cannot be lost in narrative: `▶️ RUN THIS` (a command is coming — one block, what it does, what to expect, what to tell Alex after), `⚠️ DECISION NEEDED` (one recommendation, not a menu), `⛔ BLOCKED` (what, why, who can clear it), `📌 FYI` (no action). Always one space after the emoji (operator 2026-09-18: the first info glyph rendered like an upside-down exclamation mark — glyphs must be unambiguous in a terminal). Everything else is narrative and may be skipped. Interim (effective immediately, behavioral): α uses these four headers in chat from 2026-09-18; the epic makes it enforced (a Stop-hook lint over the final assistant message: a fenced `!` command without a `▶ RUN THIS` header, or a question without `⚠ DECISION NEEDED`, is flagged).
  4. **Executive register by default.** Operator 2026-09-18: "You're acting like a low-level employee just dumping masses of information on me… treat me as the founder/executive… assume he's pretty busy." The President reports like a chief of staff: bottom line first, ≤5 short lines by default, plain words, no internal ids/SHAs/ruling numbers unless asked; detail lives in a file the operator CAN open, never in the chat. Applies to the product too — Master Console's users get the same register. Enforced by the same Stop-hook lint (length + jargon budget on operator-facing messages; override only when the operator asks for detail).
- **Decision loop (the part that makes it a SYSTEM, not a dashboard):** detector findings land in a `process-findings` store → at session start and at sprint retro the President reads the top-N by cost → each gets exactly one of: FIX NOW (bounded), ROADMAP (via `/roadmap:add`), RULE CHANGE (CLAUDE.md / agent spec, with its enforcer named), or ACCEPT (with the measured cost recorded). No finding is silently dropped; "ACCEPT" is a decision with a name on it.
- **Structural fixes already known (first sprint candidates):** (a) lead-yield discipline — the lead never holds a turn >10 min while teammates are conducting; long lead work is delegated or chunked (mechanical: a PostToolUse timer that warns the lead when teammate inbox depth >0 and the turn is >N min old); (b) verdict-to-file — every in-process reviewer WRITES its verdict JSON to a named path so records never depend on hand-back routing; (c) β-row writer — the verdict row is appended at receipt by a script the consumer can run, not by whoever happens to be free; (d) rules-not-rulings — the lead issues a decision RULE at sprint start (fix-or-residual, lead-silence) so conductors are not serialized on per-item rulings (R-124, 2026-09-18).
- **Priority:** ⭐ HIGH — operator 2026-09-18: "This is supposed to be a hands-off autonomous development system. It is not very hands-off and it's not very autonomous." **State:** Planned (DISCUSSED-2026-09-18). **Completion:** 0%.
- **Epic tracker:** not yet minted — `/epic:plan` after E-OPEN-SOURCE-001 closes; sequence WITH E-LEAN-GAUNTLET-001 (shared telemetry: the gauntlet package selector's budget events are this epic's first detector input).
- **Dependencies:** logging rebuild (operator P1, ED-066) for trustworthy event timestamps; `paths.recurringIssues`. **Rationale:** every slow point named in E-LEAN-GAUNTLET-001 was discovered by the OPERATOR getting frustrated, not by the system. **Expected impact:** the system surfaces "this is slow / this keeps breaking" itself, with numbers, and proposes the fix.
- **Enforcer (named at write-time):** a session-end gate that refuses a clean wrap when budget-overrun or recurrence≥3 findings exist without a recorded decision; the message-contract Stop-hook lint. Until built: enforcement debt at epic mint.
- **Related definitions:** Enforcer, Event, Epic, Recurring issue.

### Parked epics (trigger-gated — no current commitment)

Architectural bets parked behind explicit revival triggers; no sprint cycles and no epic tracker file until the trigger fires (then they graduate to Planned). Full design notes under `### Later (trigger-gated)` + `## Later: Platform Bets` below.
- **E-CENTRAL-MC** — central multi-product architecture. *Trigger:* updates cost >30 min/week, or cross-product orchestration/reporting/shared-memory becomes a recurring need, or new-product setup is still painful after E-MULTIPRODUCT-001. *(RFC: `_docs/research/2026-05-21-central-mc-rfc.md`.)*
- **E-NPM-DISTRIBUTION** — npm distribution as forcing function. *Trigger:* most active meta-work would be unnecessary under the npm shape. *(Essay: `_docs/research/2026-05-19-npm-forcing-function.md`.)*
- **E-MC-DOGFOOD** — MC-as-product deep dogfooding. *Trigger:* cadence-rule violations stay at 0 for 4+ consecutive sprint windows (boundary closure first).
- **E-PROMPT-LIBRARY** — reusable saved operating-prompt library. *Trigger:* the operator re-pastes the same operating rules across ≥3 sessions, or the `session:dump` reusable-prompt block gets hand-copied more than a couple of times. *(DISCUSSED-2026-06-06.)*
- **E-NATIVE-PACKAGING-001** — native app scaffolds for `bootstrap:spinup --where`. *Goal:* `scripts/scaffold/app.js` grows platform-conditional scaffolds — Expo/React Native for `android|ios`, Tauri/Electron for `desktop-pc|desktop-mac` — so `--where` produces a native baseline, not just web/PWA. *Trigger:* a portfolio product actually targets a native platform (until then the web/PWA baseline + honest target-recording is the v1, per `MC.md` WG-1). *Source:* MC-PROMPT.md §3 (created 2026-06-06 by E-SPINUP-STEPS-001).
- **E-AGENT-DISCIPLINE** — convenient-path-over-rules forcing function. *Goal:* a **forcing function — not more doctrine** — that stops the agent reaching for the convenient path instead of (a) grounding in truth first, (b) following the already-built skill, and (c) respecting the existing guards. These lapses are self-inflicted and strongest at the highest-trust (autonomous) moments, and they erode operator trust ("why is opus 4.8 constantly breaking the rules here"). The existing CLAUDE.md rules ("ground assumptions in truth", "prefer existing skills", respect the guards) did **not** prevent it. *Cases — 2026-06-07 launch-research session (the examples):* **(1)** declared the OpenAI/Gemini API keys "missing" and substituted a weaker approach **without opening `.env.local`**, where all three keys sat (operator: "did you not see api keys in an env file…?"); **(2)** hand-rolled a single-call deep-research runner instead of reading `research:deep` and following its documented **progressive 4-phase** design (Landscape→Mechanics→Failure-Modes→Contrarian), so the operator had to correct it; **(3)** reached for **`node -e fs.writeFileSync`** to append a ledger — the *exact* shortcut `merge-guard.js` blocks — minutes after writing that same gap up as `MC.md` **WG-2**. *Direction:* a pre-action **grounding gate** for a few high-frequency reflexes (verify-before-declaring-unavailable · skill-scan-before-reinventing-a-procedure · honor-the-guard) + **convenient-path telemetry** surfaced at `/scan:full`; strengthen `SUGGESTED SKILLS` salience so skills are *invoked*, not reimplemented; log an enforcement-debt entry if no mechanical detector ships (per CLAUDE.md § Policy & Enforcement Hygiene). *Trigger:* the pattern has already recurred 3× in one session — flagged to **graduate to a Planned epic + tracker** at the next agent/skill-reliability sprint. *Cross-ref:* `MC.md` WG-2 (the `node -e` guard), CLAUDE.md § Reasoning / Skill Use / Policy & Enforcement Hygiene. *Source:* operator-directed `/roadmap:add` 2026-06-07. (DISCUSSED-2026-06-07.)

The version-themed milestone numbers here (`0.10.0`/`0.12.0`/`0.13.0`/`0.14.0`/`0.16.0`/`0.17.0`/`0.18.0`) had **drifted from the actual release tags**, so epics use semantic IDs decoupled from release versions. **This section is preserved for history and as the detail behind each epic** (each epic's `Detail (deprecated-milestone source)` field points back here). Each milestone named *what shifts in the engineering reality* of the framework; the sprints listed beneath it were the units of work that closed it. *(This rhythm now lives at the epic granularity — see `## Epics`.)*
