**[open] Verify the design system + the requirements-system-across-modes are actually wired (audit-first — both may already be done).** *(operator ask 2026-06-03)* Two adjacent "confirm, then close deltas" audits surfaced while reviewing the agent system for the rewrite:
1. **Design system (UI/UX framework + tokens).** On-disk reality is **substantially built**: the scaffold lays an OSS stack — **Next.js + Tailwind v4 + shadcn/ui + Radix + Lucide** (`framework/templates/app-scaffold/`), tokens as CSS custom properties in `src/app/globals.css` (`@theme inline` → utilities, no raw hex), the per-product design spec at `_requirements/01-design-system/{COLOR_SEMANTICS,COMPONENT_LIBRARY,UX_PRINCIPLES}.md`, the static gate `scan:design-system` (+ `ui-lint.js` hook), the **design-quality gauntlet** (`design-quality` + `visual-review`, Claude-pinned for Playwright MCP), and the design **knowledge** in `_guides/design/` (~18 principle topics). **Verify:** docs↔scaffold token parity (`TOKENS.md`/`FEEDBACK_PATTERNS.md` are referenced by some agents but absent from canonical `01-design-system/`), and that design judgment being **single-provider** (Claude-only, MCP-bound) is acceptable. **Rewrite tie:** `_guides/design` → `_knowledge/design` migration (plan M3); Design Lead owns it.
2. **Requirements system (born in oneshot) wired into the other modes.** The `_requirements/` tree + the requirements reviewers exist in **both** `01-adhoc` and `02-oneshot` (`req-reviewer`, `compliance`); `build-core.md` is shared FE/BE discipline across both modes; builders/reviewers in both read `_requirements/01-design-system` + `04-features`. **Verify:** that adhoc (and the future **ε sprint** mode) consume the same requirement/traceability pipeline oneshot produces — no oneshot-only gap. **Rewrite tie:** this IS the **mode-agnostic-workers + requirement-pipeline** design (one role spec per worker; mode = context the orchestrator passes) + folding `req-reviewer`/`compliance` into **QA Reviewer scopes** under the Quality Lead. Full spec: `runtime/notes/agent-system-rewrite-plan.md`; master plan: `DUMP.md`.
Priority: low — both are largely built; this is a confirm-and-close-deltas pass, mostly subsumed by the agent-system rewrite. Tracked here so it isn't lost if the rewrite slips.

**[open] Epics over milestones + a persistent, per-task-state work tracker.** *(operator ask 2026-06-05)* Reorganize the planning unit from **milestones** (the current `## 🏛 Milestones` rhythm — "n sprints → milestone hit") toward **epics**, and stand up a **persistent tracker of those epics with clear state per task and a checkmark for finally-done**. Two shapes to weigh: (a) **retool `DUMP.md`** — today an *ephemeral* single-session prescriptive handoff (`/session:dump` overwrites it each session; gitignored) — into a *durable*, session-surviving epic/task tracker; or (b) build the tracker **into the sprint system** (an epic layer over `paths.sprintActiveRegistry` + the per-sprint state already kept). **Why now:** answering the operator's "genuinely what's left of the rewrite?" this session took **two reconstruction audit-agents** to re-derive scope scattered across the ephemeral `DUMP.md`, `paths.enforcementDebt` (ED-021..ED-026), and `runtime/notes/agent-system-rewrite-plan.md` — there is no single *living* tracker with per-item done-state, so "what's left" is an investigation, not a glance. **Design questions to settle first:** (1) epic-vs-milestone semantics — milestones already name "what shifts in engineering reality"; are epics a rename, a finer grain *beneath* milestones, or a replacement? (2) tracker home — retool `DUMP.md` vs a sprint-system layer vs a new `paths`-registered artifact — and how it relates to `## 🏛 Milestones`, `paths.sprintActiveRegistry`, and `paths.enforcementDebt` (don't mint a 4th drifting tracker). (3) the **named enforcer** (CLAUDE.md § Policy & Enforcement Hygiene) — a per-task-state tracker can silently rot, so it needs a coverage check in the `scan:scan-coverage` / `guides:coverage` mold. Immediate test case = the agent-system rewrite's own remainder (M1-c tail → M1-d → ED-024). Priority: medium — a planning/visibility ergonomic (high leverage for multi-epic work the operator steps away from), not a correctness blocker.

**[open] Simplify the `.claude/agents/` "system" folder naming — `.system/` vs `_system/` vs `.system.md` collide.** *(operator ask 2026-06-05)* Post-ADR-0007 the agents tree carries THREE different "system" conventions at/under `.claude/agents/`, which is confusing to navigate: (a) **`.system/`** — a hidden folder at the agents root (`frontmatter-guide.md` + `guides/`); (b) **`.system.md`** — a 72KB file at the agents root (the "Agent System — Operational Specification" doc); (c) **`_system/`** — a department-style folder holding the system-tier worker agents (`learner.md`, `stub-scaffold.md`); plus (d) **`president/_system/`** — the president faces' framework-internals folder (policy/ADRs/adhoc+oneshot protocols/lexicon/schema). Three names (`.system` dotted-hidden · `_system` underscored · `.system.md` file) for three different meanings of "system." **Fix direction (decide first, then sweep):** pick ONE convention + a clear semantic split — e.g. keep `_system/` as the system-tier *agent home* (consistent with the `_org/` / `_principles/` siblings), and give the root `.system/` + `.system.md` reference/internals content a distinct non-colliding name (a `_reference/`, or fold into `president/_system/`), so "system" means exactly one thing per location. **Constraints:** multi-ref rename across hash-tracked paths (agent specs, both manifests, the paths registry, any hooks/skills referencing these dirs) — do it as its OWN sprint with a full OLD-path grep-sweep (rename-hygiene bug class; cf. the `scripts/check/`→`scripts/checks/` consolidation in "Scan-suite reconciliation" above), regen all three manifests, and add/extend a coverage check so the new convention can't silently drift. Priority: low-medium — legibility/maintainability, not correctness; pure post-rewrite cleanup.

**[open] `agents:init` skill — tool agents per-project at scaffold time.** *(operator ask 2026-06-09)* Today the canonical agents (esp. the security pod) carry product-STACK assumptions (the Jobzooka Next.js/extension/Rocket-billing leak — see **Now: Framework Boundary & Identity → "Security agent specs bake in a product STACK"**). This is the *mechanism* that entry's per-project fix calls for: a new `agents:init` skill that runs during project scaffolding (`/portfolio:new`, `/bootstrap:spinup`, `/mc:setup`) and TOOLS each agent for the SPECIFIC project being built — detect/declare the product's stack (framework, language, dir layout, auth model, billing, data layer) and parameterize each agent's detection logic + scopes accordingly. So e.g. the security-reviewer's route-scanner / secret-scanner / config-auditor / nextjs-cve personas check the RIGHT paths + patterns for THIS product (Next.js vs Remix vs FastAPI vs Rails vs …), not a hardcoded canonical stack; backend/frontend builders get the project's real `src` layout; design-quality gets the project's design-system path. Canonical agents stay GENERIC (grounded in the framework-generic `_knowledge/` library); `agents:init` writes the per-project agent-tooling config the agents read at dispatch. Design Qs: where the per-project config lives (a project manifest the dispatch layer merges into the agent prompt), stack detection (operator-declared vs auto-sniffed from package.json/deps/dir-layout), and whether it re-runs on `/mc:update` (new agents added upstream need tooling). Pairs with + implements the Framework-Boundary purity entry; the `/scan:framework-purity` agent-spec-leak check (also logged there) is the enforcer that keeps canonical generic once this lands. *(DISCUSSED-2026-06-09)*

**[open] Feature lifecycle state — design for cold→warm→repeat-return, not just the cold/demo state.** *(operator ask 2026-06-20)* Features pass through a lifecycle of states as they're used — **cold** (brand-new / empty), **cold→first-session-complete**, **first-return**, and **repeat-return(s)** — and a design that only works in the cold/demo state can degrade badly in the accreted warm/repeat state. Canonical example: a user action that appends to a list looks fine cold, but after many sessions the list grows enormous and the UI/UX breaks down. Every such surface needs a deliberate answer chosen AT DESIGN TIME, not patched after it bloats — paginate/virtualize, collapse/summarize, archive/age-out, relocate, or cap+overflow. **Fix direction:** make per-feature state-over-time a first-class spec/design concern: (1) a **lifecycle-state checklist** in the PRD/STORIES standards (`_requirements/_standards/*`) — for each feature, name its behavior + UX at cold / first-complete / first-return / repeat-return, and flag any unbounded-growth / accretion surface with its containment strategy; (2) a **design-quality gauntlet axis** — "does this hold up at repeat-return / high-volume state?" (the gauntlet today reviews the rendered cold state). Pairs with the **"App navigation planning + persistent UI/UX knowledge system"** entry (Later: Platform Bets) — both address product UX degrading over use. Finer-grained than, and complementary to, the product-phase lifecycle model (Research→Early-Dev→Launch→Finding-PMF→PMF): that's the product's arc; this is each feature's state-over-time. *(DISCUSSED-2026-06-20)*

**[open] Deeper-W4 — finish removing Jobzooka's product VOCABULARY from canonical (W4 Option-A follow-on).** *(2026-06-22)* W4 RESTRUCTURE (`c89a73a7`) made canonical `_requirements/` Jobzooka-NAME-clean (relocated the filled instance → `_mc/EXAMPLES/Jobzooka/`), but per the operator's Option-A scope it left Jobzooka's DOMAIN VOCABULARY behind — same product, identified by jargon not name. Three residuals to sweep (judgment-heavy — do NOT blind-sweep; some tokens may be legit framework vocab): **(1) ~19 canonical files** carry rockets / Scout-Strike-Arsenal tiers / $X.99 pricing / Deus-Mechanicus / masterResume etc. — incl. the `FIELD_REGISTRY.json` keystone (needs a template-vs-instance call) + 5 arch contracts (USER/PAYMENT/PERMISSIONS/SESSION/DATA-CONTRACTS) + `04-stripe.md` + a `gate-dodger.spec.ts` + `COVERAGE.md` + 6 `_audits`; **(2) `_mc/BASELINE/_requirements/`** is still a full Jobzooka copy (38 files) — archive → `_mc/EXAMPLES/Jobzooka/BASELINE/` or delete; **(3) `_mc/EXAMPLES/`** is not yet in the framework-manifest ship-walk — wire it if downstream products should receive the labeled example (today it's canonical-only). The `framework-purity` guard treats this vocab as ADVISORY (non-blocking) by design, so it won't false-green — but the mission goal ("canonical carries NO baked-in product") isn't fully met until the vocab is swept. Own its own sprint (per-file judgment). *(DISCUSSED-2026-06-22)*

**[open] `/portfolio:new` multi-template support — the scaffolder has ONE frozen template and it's a web app.** *(operator ask 2026-08-10 — "more templated lol")* Today `/portfolio:new` pours the same Next.js + React + Supabase web-app starter into EVERY new product, without asking what kind of product it is. First non-web product (`vlad`, a plain-node Agent-SDK engine + MCP stdio server) received ~2,280 web-scaffold files, and the mismatch cost real judgment calls mid-build: the web dependency tree fought the product's credential-custody dependency-surface residual (ADR-0041 A1), the scaffold's playwright test lane was wrong for a no-UI product, Supabase auth is a hosted shape the epic ruled out of scope, and `PROJECT.md` shipped as an unfilled template. Resolution used for vlad (E-VLAD-001 D-3): engine as a self-contained `engine/` subtree with its own package.json, web scaffold recorded dormant — plus the operator's 2026-08-10 follow-up ruling: strip the unneeded scaffold from vlad and reapply a fitting template (scheduled at the epic, post-W1-merge, so the in-flight build surface isn't disturbed). **Fix direction (MC-side):** (1) `/portfolio:new` asks or accepts a product-shape flag (`--template web|engine|minimal`) at scaffold time; (2) add at least one minimal/plain-engine template (plain node, `node --test` lane, no hosted-auth deps, near-zero-dep posture); (3) a documented retrofit path ("reapply a fitting template" for an existing product — vlad is the test case); (4) fold in the already-flagged template gap that `.claude/settings.json` ships `Write(path)` allow-rules the harness ignores for file tools (`Edit(path)` is the honored form — ε MC.md flag, 2026-08-04). **Enforcer candidate (per Policy & Enforcement Hygiene):** a scaffold-time check that the chosen template's test lane + dependency posture match the declared product shape, and that no template ships permission rules in a form the harness won't honor. Pairs with the `agents:init` per-project tooling entry above — both are "scaffold to the ACTUAL product, not the historical default." *(DISCUSSED-2026-08-10)*

Add a catalog-validation check that periodically pings declared models with a 1-token prompt and flags 404s. *(DISCOVERED-2026-05-11)*

Diversity preserved either way (both Google). *(DISCOVERED-2026-05-11)*

**[open] Spec-propagation closer.** Walk dependent spec nodes via SPEC_GRAPH on `/scan:requirements drift`; surface downstream specs that MUST update; fail gauntlet until propagation attested. Design separately before implementation.

**[in-progress] Tracker hygiene — superseded by full purge.** Earlier plan was to deprecate `mc-to-update.md` in canonical and keep it in products as a local `/mc:flag` ledger. 2026-05-22 decision: full purge instead — `mc-to-update.md`, `/mc:flag`, `/warp:promote`, `/warp:promote-flags` are all being deleted from canonical and from products. See **Now: Framework Boundary & Identity → "Full purge of the upstream-discovery surface"** for the comprehensive deletion plan. Only remaining hygiene work here: `/roadmap:add` matures into the canonical-side discovery surface (write directly to the relevant ROADMAP subsection with lifecycle tags).

**[open] `/session:write` may broadcast stale content.** The auto-gather step (git diff/log time-windows, last-task inference, learnings/systems-delta detection) risks surfacing a *prior* session's or otherwise stale content on a write — e.g. a 2-hour git-log window or last-task inference under compaction can attribute another session's work to this one. Verify the gathered context reflects ONLY the current session, and fix attribution if confirmed. Affected: `.claude/commands/session/write.md`. *(DISCOVERED-2026-05-31)*

**[open] Smart gauntlet-necessity classifier.** Today the gauntlet-skip decision is a manual speed-cadence lever ("skip the gauntlet when low-risk" — tickets ≤ medium risk with a green e2e skip the reviewer/qa/redteam gauntlet; high/critical keep it). Promote it to an automatic determination: classify each build's risk from real signals — diff surface (enforcer/gate/auth/dispatch/release code → always full gauntlet), files/lines touched, the ticket's `risk` field, and whether it touches a known false-green-prone class — and auto-route to full-gauntlet vs. lightweight-review vs. skip instead of the orchestrator guessing per build. **Fail-safe:** unknown/ambiguous → full gauntlet (enforcer-class lanes are exactly where cross-provider review keeps catching real false-greens; cf. SP-20260602-001 keystone + the recurring false-green class). Pairs with the cost motivation: the full 4-reviewer cross-provider gauntlet is the long pole on every enforcer sprint, so skipping it *safely* when genuinely low-risk is real leverage — but only with a trustworthy classifier + explicit operator override. Design the classifier + override surface before implementation. *(DISCUSSED-2026-06-02)*

**[open] Worktree-dispatch torture test.** A deliberate stress/torture harness for the isolated-worktree build-dispatch path — the failure class that recurs under load (RI-004: the harness auto-backgrounds a long builder/fixer dispatch then silently reaps it → 0-byte output, no completion record, orchestrator waits forever; lived twice on SP-20260602-001, where the keystone builder reaped under Gamma and Alpha took the build foreground). Cover: many concurrent worktree dispatches; long-running (> auto-background-threshold) dispatches; mid-dispatch reap simulation; completion-record-in-worktree-vs-canonical cwd resolution (ED-016); worktree cleanup after a reap; `git worktree` lock/contention; and the chunk-on-reap + savepoint-early recovery actually holding. Goal: turn RI-004's "working mitigation" (chunk + bounded dispatch + savepoint-after-each + prefer dispatch-agent.js for completion records) into an ENFORCED, tested contract — a bounded-dispatch wrapper with a hard timeout + chunk ceiling + >Nmin-quiet+0-output=dead auto-redispatch, proven by the torture suite. *(DISCUSSED-2026-06-02)*

**[open] Wire the design guides into the BUILDERS + bootstrap pipeline (not just the reviewers).** Today the 19 `_guides/design/` guides (commit cb33f0a) are wired into the 4 DESIGNER agents (product-designer, web-conversion-designer, design-quality, visual-review) — the REVIEW/judgment side — via `<!-- DESIGN-GUIDES -->` blocks, `anchor:none` (grounding only). The gap: they are NOT wired into the frontend-BUILDER agents (01-adhoc/02-oneshot frontend-builder) that actually GENERATE UI, nor into the bootstrap pipeline (`/bootstrap:spinup` / `/portfolio:new`) as live steps (the `/guides:integrate` machinery exists for exactly this). So the guides shape how UI is JUDGED but not how it's BUILT. Wire them into the builders' context + the spinup/lastmile anchors so the principles guide generation, then enforce via `/guides:coverage`. Pairs with the held MASTERPLAN W2 items (design-lead role + component-library auto-scaffold). *(DISCUSSED-2026-06-02)*

**[open] Enforce lean-dispatch for heavy aggregate/verify/research skills (generalize the §2.5 context lever beyond the build-chain).** The dispatch-context lever — orchestrator dispatches heavy work to a sub-agent that WRITES a file + RETURNS only a lean envelope, never reading/aggregating the full output in its own context — is enforced today ONLY for build-chain roles (`scripts/hooks/dispatch-route-guard.js` §2.5 Agent-tool gate). The same bloat class hits heavy AGGREGATE/VERIFY/RESEARCH skills: `/scan:full` (~30 sub-scans), `/research:deep`, `/redteam:full`, `/qa:audit`, big multi-source synthesis — each is "high token cost + a reducible verdict," so running the aggregation in the orchestrator's own context is exactly the skipped-lever leak (operator threat-level concern 2026-06-04; proven the correct pattern this session — the ADR-0007 cutover ship-gate, the 25-spec build, and the cutover convergence all ran as dispatched lean-return agents and kept the orchestrator's context flat). Deliverable: (a) bake a "heavy-skill dispatch contract" into those skill bodies so the lean-verdict return (`{scans, criticals, decision: SHIP|BLOCKED}`) is by-construction, not optional; (b) extend the §2.5 gate (or a sibling scan) to flag a heavy skill run in-orchestrator instead of dispatched. Until the gate ships, tracked as enforcement-debt (`/enforcement:list`). Source: RT-2026-06-04-dispatch-context-lever + memory `feedback-orchestrator-holds-envelopes-not-content`. *(DISCUSSED-2026-06-04)*

**[open] System-wide token-optimization pass — generalize "run skills isolated" beyond the heavy-aggregate set.** ED-021 (the heavy-skill lean-return contract) is now SHIPPED (E8, `dispatch-route-guard.js#findHeavySkillAdvisory` + the §2.5 build-chain gate, `db0a778`) — it proved the pattern: dispatch heavy work to a sub-agent that WRITES a file + RETURNS only a lean envelope, keeping the orchestrator context flat (`/learn:deep` is the canonical model — it fans out per-source extractor sub-agents). The broader work the operator wants: AUDIT THE ENTIRE SYSTEM for token reduction — build a token-cost map of the skill catalog + the agent dispatch paths, identify which skills/operations should run ISOLATED (dispatched lean-return), which should FAN OUT in parallel, and which read/aggregate too much in their own context; convert the high-cost ones; measure before/after. Proven this session: E5–E8 ran as three parallel dispatched sprints + every scan-gate as a lean-return agent, keeping orchestrator context flat across the whole rewrite. Pairs with the §2.5 lean-dispatch entry above + memory `feedback-orchestrator-holds-envelopes-not-content`. *(DISCUSSED-2026-06-05)*

**[open] Encode "bias to DO over defer (when context allows)" as an operating principle.** The recurring failure mode the operator named (2026-06-05): deferring work that should just be done in-session ("deferred this, deferred that" — felt-progress chronically lags real-progress across sessions, cf. ED-027). The discipline: when context + safety genuinely allow, slap through the work rather than logging a deferral — proven this session, the 61-ref cutover cleanup was DONE, not deferred, once E8's gate could verify it (operator: "sometimes we defer when we should just slap through the work… genuinely produces better output"). Encode it: (a) a β judgment-model posture + decision-policy completeness bar — *a deferral must justify WHY-not-now (irreversibility · missing input · genuine scope-cut), not convenience*; (b) consider a light flag for convenience-deferrals (the judgment inverse of ED-027/ED-028). NOT "never defer" — defer when context is exhausted or the work is genuinely out of scope; the bias is toward action on the close calls. *(DISCUSSED-2026-06-05)*

**[open] Optimize the prompt + context injector (`smart-context.js`).** The per-prompt pipeline (`scripts/hooks/smart-context.js`) runs on every UserPromptSubmit: it sends the prompt + memory stores to Haiku, which enriches the prompt and selects relevant memory items as `additionalContext` (fail-open). It has never been tuned as a system, and a knob change this session moved the bottleneck: the learnings STORE cap was raised 100→1000 (keep more signal), but RECALL still loads only the recent 60 (`MAX_LEARNINGS=60` in `smart-context.js`) and surfaces 5–7 per prompt — so recall is **recency-biased, not relevance-ranked from the full pool**; the older ~940 learnings exist but are never surfaced. The work: (1) **relevance-ranked retrieval from the FULL store** (keyword/embedding rank over all learnings/traces/decisions, not `slice(-60)`), so the *right* item surfaces regardless of age; (2) **selection precision** — measure the hit-rate of what Haiku injects (is it the relevant few, or noise?), tune the prompt; (3) **payload + latency tuning** — the 15000ms Haiku timeout + per-source caps (60 learnings / 20 traces / 20 decisions) vs. recall breadth; (4) **caching** — avoid re-running the Haiku enrichment on every prompt when context is unchanged (the 0.17.0 hook-overhaul's "smart-context caching #3"); (5) **enrichment-quality measurement** — does the injected context actually improve responses, or is it ignored? Pairs with the learnings-cap raise (the store now holds the signal; recall must surface it) + the 0.17.0 hook-system overhaul. Affected: `scripts/hooks/smart-context.js` (`MAX_LEARNINGS`, the selection prompt, the payload caps). *(DISCUSSED-2026-06-06)*

**[shipped — 2026-06-06] Mode-entry must NOT trigger autonomous work (mode-init ≠ authorization).** Entering a mode — `/mode:sprint`, `/mode:adhoc`, `/mode:oneshot`, `/mode:solo` — must ONLY set up the mode (write the marker, spawn the persistent team, apply turbo if requested) and then STOP and await an explicit task. It must NOT proceed into work, even when a prior session's handoff/DUMP/TRACKER says "continue" or names a forward plan. **This session's failure:** `/mode:sprint /session:turbo` — operator intent was *initialize the mode* — was read, together with the prior DUMP's "continue / execute the forward plan," as a green light, and Alex ran a full reconcile → verify → commit → `/session:end` cycle the operator never asked for. Mode setup is plumbing; a handoff is context, not a command. **Fix options:** (a) the `/mode:*` skills END at the "mode active — give me a task" confirmation and explicitly do NOT chain into `/sprint:full` / build / "continue"; (b) treat an inherited "continue" from a DUMP/handoff as REQUIRING an explicit in-session operator go before any state-changing action (pairs with ED-028 inherited-claim provenance); (c) a guard/posture making mode-entry inert — the first state-changing action after a bare `/mode:*` needs an operator instruction in the SAME session, not just the handoff. Behavioral at minimum (mode-skill body + α posture); consider a mechanical gate (PostToolUse on mode-set, or an α-loop precondition). **Shipped (2026-06-06, session/2026-06-06):** all three layers landed — (mechanical) `scripts/mode-set.js`, the single canonical writer every `/mode:*` skill calls, now prints a loud "⛔ MODE-INIT ≠ AUTHORIZATION — setup only, STOP and await an explicit in-session task" banner on every *fresh* mode entry (silent on same-mode lock/activeBuild re-runs; tested); (behavioral) all four `/mode:*` skills gained a top-of-Procedure STOP section and `sprint.md`'s "Run the sprint" step is re-gated as a separate, task-triggered action explicitly NOT reached by mode entry; (doctrine) `CLAUDE.md` + `alpha.md` carry a "Mode-init ≠ authorization" rule scoping "Act, don't ask" (an inherited "continue" is context, not a command). The residual — no mechanical *detector* of a violation (a hard first-action gate is high-risk/low-value: it would block the legitimate `/mode:X <task>` case) — is logged as **ED-031** (`/enforcement:list`). *(REPORTED-2026-06-06 → SHIPPED-2026-06-06)*

**[open] Repeated full re-gauntlets are inefficient — explore alternatives.** S-LC-01 ran 3 fix-cycles, each followed by a FULL re-gauntlet (both cross-provider lanes re-reviewing the ENTIRE diff), each finding exactly 1 deeper bug (3→2→1 — healthy convergence, but expensive). Two concrete wastes observed: (a) the backend-reviewer PASSed on passes 2 AND 3 yet was re-dispatched on the full diff each time; (b) every re-review re-read the whole diff, not just the fix delta. Alternatives to explore: (1) **diff-scoped re-review** — after a fix-cycle, re-review only the changed files / the specific prior findings, not the full diff; (2) **skip already-PASSed lanes** — don't re-dispatch a lane whose scope is satisfied and whose covered inputs didn't change (a PASS is sticky until the files it covered change); (3) **deeper single first-pass** — invest in a thorough class-finding pass up front (higher effort, or an explicit "find ALL instances of this class" instruction) so sibling/class bugs surface in ONE review instead of one-per-cycle; (4) **cheap deterministic pre-gauntlet lints** — fast local checks (e.g. "no raw registry read", "no hardcoded roster literal") that catch the class before the expensive cross-provider gauntlet; (5) **fix-the-class-not-the-instance briefs** (partially adopted in S-LC-01 fix#2/#3 — sweeping the whole class cut cycles). Tradeoff to weigh: re-reviewing only the delta risks missing an interaction the fix introduced elsewhere — so diff-scoping needs a "blast-radius-of-the-fix" guard (don't blindly trust a sticky PASS if the fix touched a shared file the passed lane covered). Cost signal: each full re-gauntlet = 2 cross-provider (gpt-5.5) reviews; 3 cycles = 6 reviews where ~2–3 would have sufficed. Relates to E-LIFECYCLE-001 (sprint discipline / dispatch-shape) — could fold there or stand alone. *(DISCUSSED-2026-06-09)*

**[open] Custom confidence requirements per Beta decision type.** Today every Beta verdict carries a confidence score (0–1) but the system treats them all the same regardless of WHAT is being decided — a 0.88 `DECIDE` that proceeds a build and a 0.88 verdict that *descopes* the plan clear the same (nonexistent) bar. The operator wants per-decision-type confidence FLOORS so the required confidence scales with the stakes + direction of the call. **Decision types observed** (β `events.jsonl`, 106 entries — `.claude/agents/president/_system/beta/events.jsonl`): *verdict types* = `DECIDE` (≈16, the overwhelming default), `DIRECTIVE` (resequence / HOW-guidance), `ESCALATE` (1 — the only operator-surfacing path); *decision classes* = A (implementation / reversible), B (meaningful-technical — ≈16 of 17 recent; e.g. this session's five sprint-boundary verdicts all `DECIDE B 0.89–0.92`), C (strategic / irreversible — 1 observed); *action shapes* = proceed-as-planned · reshape/resequence · descope/simplify · defer/un-decide · risk-name-only. **Times the operator disagreed-with / constrained Beta** (the evidence the thresholds must encode): (1) **hardening-phase no-defer** — operator 2026-06-11 ruled β must NOT defer or un-decide anything on risk grounds, HOW-guidance + risk-naming only (P-064 / ED-044) → a *defer* verdict is effectively floored at 1.0 (disallowed) during hardening; (2) **≥0.95 scope-reduction floor** — operator 2026-06-11 ruled β may reduce scope from an existing plan ONLY at ≥0.95 confidence, else α auto-overrides (ED-046, memory `feedback_beta_no_regress_locked_build`); (3) the standing **"advisors water down locked work"** pattern — operator has repeatedly overridden scope-reducing β verdicts on design-locked builds (same memory). **Proposed starter matrix** (action-shape → min confidence): proceed / HOW-guidance → 0.6 (cheap default); reshape / resequence → 0.8; descope / simplify → **0.95** (operator-set); defer / un-decide → **disallowed during hardening, else 0.9**; any Class-C-touching call → `ESCALATE` regardless of confidence. **Enforcer:** extend Tier-4 β-honesty (`scan:sprint-beta-honesty`) to read each verdict's {shape, class, confidence} and flag any verdict that *acts* below its floor (the descope-below-0.95 check is ED-046; generalize it to the full matrix). Thresholds live in the β judgment-model / decision-policy as DATA (tunable per phase — hardening vs. normal), not prose. *(DISCUSSED-2026-06-11)*

**[open] `/session:resume` hardening for long-running autonomous sprint sessions.** Make resume reliable for overnight-style work — explicit mode restoration, fully-authorized push/merge sessions, runtime-failure recovery, and tracker-drift handling — via new modifiers: `/session:resume --turbo --mode sprint --long-run --scope push-to-main --runtime-watch --tracker-reconcile`. **New flags:** **`--mode <mode>`** — explicitly re-establish the requested mode instead of relying on handoff inference; for `--mode sprint`, materially restore sprint state (mode marker, α+ε+β team, stale-agent reconciliation, readiness pings) *then execute*. **`--long-run`** — continue across multiple sprint/checkpoint cycles; don't end after one sprint/commit/merge/checkpoint; durably checkpoint at boundaries (`/session:checkpoint`); only end on explicit user request, context/tool limits, no safe autonomous work remaining, or a hard safety/human gate. **`--scope push-to-main`** — treat the invocation as explicit push/merge authorization for this session (incl. to `main`) while preserving hard safety floors (no force-push, no deleting human work, no secret exposure, no destructive prod/data/billing without approval, no unresolved β `ESCALATE`). **`--runtime-watch`** — detect + handle transient runtime failures (Claude/provider outages, agent stalls, worker crashes, tool/CLI errors, network timeouts, 429/quota, 5xx/gateway, context/tool interruptions, partial command failures): classify → retry-with-backoff-where-safe → continue unblocked work instead of idling → periodically recheck blocked deps → use valid fallback models/tools/mocks → preserve state before risky retries → resume the blocked path when available → record unresolved blockers in durable state. **`--tracker-reconcile`** — treat `TRACKER.md` as important-but-not-infallible; reconcile against `DUMP.md`, handoff/checkpoint files, git state, active sprint/epic + roadmap files, and actual repo evidence; if stale/contradictory, repair the tracker FIRST instead of letting stale state override newer evidence (pairs with verify-don't-inherit / ED-056). **Key reconciliation:** this intentionally extends the shipped "mode-init ≠ authorization" rule above — a bare `/mode:sprint` still STOPs, but an explicit `/session:resume --mode sprint --long-run` typed *this session* IS the in-session authorization to proceed; the docs must state both without conflict. **AC:** (1) `--mode sprint` reliably restores sprint mode AND does not stop after mode-init; (2) `--long-run` keeps selecting + executing the next safe roadmap/sprint work after each checkpoint; (3) `--scope push-to-main` removes unnecessary push/merge prompts while preserving the hard gates; (4) `--runtime-watch` survives transient failures (rate-limit/server/provider/agent-stall/tool/CLI/network) without prematurely ending; (5) `--tracker-reconcile` prevents stale-tracker poisoning of resume decisions; (6) docs for `/session:resume`, `/session:end`, `/session:end --fast`, `/mode:sprint`, and the tracker/handoff contracts are updated *together* so their instructions don't conflict; (7) tests/fixtures for: mode-not-restored, bare-sprint-setup accidentally stopping execution, stale-tracker-vs-newer-DUMP/git, long-run continuation after the first sprint completes, push-to-main authorization, and rate-limit / server-error / stalled-agent / provider-tool-CLI recovery. **Also review** whether `/session:end --fast` should write stronger machine-readable resume metadata: intended mode, intended team, branch/worktree resume point, push/merge authorization status, long-run intent, runtime blockers, tracker confidence/drift notes. Relates to E-LIFECYCLE-001 (mode-lifecycle + session discipline) — could fold there or stand alone. *(DISCUSSED-2026-06-17)*

## Later: Platform Bets

Items parked until specific conditions change. Listed for orientation, not as a queue. Each entry names its revival trigger.

**[parked] Central-MC multi-product architecture (opt-in only).** User decision 2026-05-21: park until pull-forward trigger fires. *Trigger to pull forward* (codex stay-simple consult 2026-05-21): (1) updating MC across products regularly costs more than 30-60 min/week, OR (2) bugs are repeatedly caused by version drift between product installs, OR (3) maintainer needs cross-product orchestration / reporting / shared memory, OR (4) new-product setup remains painful AFTER install/update reliability work (Sprint 2) ships. Until then, the per-product install model is correct. Codex's design verdict: viable-with-major-caveats; ship as opt-in only, never default. Prerequisite chain (replaces the prior promote-era prerequisites): the `_mc/`-zone migration must be complete in canonical and at least 2 portfolio products, the `_mc/MANIFEST.json` schema must be stable across one minor release, and install/update reliability must be measurably boring. Hidden cost curve per codex: per-product cheap at 1-3 products, noticeable at 5, hurts at 8-12 if MC changes weekly, ops problem at 15-20. Real multiplier: `active products × framework change frequency × install drift × debugging ambiguity` — stabilize MC and 20 installs are fine. Captured as frozen RFC at `_docs/research/2026-05-21-central-mc-rfc.md`; no sprint cycles until trigger fires.

**[parked] npm distribution as forcing function.** Stand up `@mc/cli` as a parallel distribution path. Building it makes "which current sprints would be wasted under the npm shape?" unavoidable. Three integration paths (cleanest → fallback): Claude Code plugin system, symlinks (Windows-fragile), managed-mirror copy. *Trigger to pull forward:* enumerate current sprints and ask "which would be unnecessary under the npm shape?" — if "most of the meta-work" (release ledger, capsule presence, manifest honesty, ghost cleanup, the now-removed promote dance), npm has signal; if "few", canonical-clone is correct. Full essay archived at `_docs/research/2026-05-19-npm-forcing-function.md`. *(DISCUSSED-2026-05-19.)*

**[blocked] Persistent team UI + TeamCreate --force-replace (upstream Anthropic).** Claude Code does not expose a TeamCreate primitive or persistent team UI panel. `/mode:adhoc` was rewritten 2026-05-14 for honest per-call dispatch. *Trigger to pull forward:* Anthropic ships (a) team-management primitive that creates visible persistent teammates AND (b) `TeamCreate --force-replace` for refresh semantics. See `_docs/phase0/adhoc-primitive-limits.md` § "Future primitive asks". Severity: feature-gap, not a bug.

**[parked] Session recovery improvements.** Crash-recovery contract covers sprint flow but not ambient session resumption (post-`/clear`, harness restart, context compaction). *Trigger to pull forward:* a concrete failure case to anchor the design. *(DEFERRED-2026-05-18.)*

**[parked] Treat MC as a product-in-MC (deep dogfooding gate).** Write PRDs for installer, session-lifecycle, paths-resolution, hook-pipeline. Spec the Alex agent team as a feature with stories. Run `/preflight:run` + `/qa:audit` + `/redteam:full` on MC itself. *Trigger to pull forward:* product cadence is healthier per Strategy cadence rule. Defer until then; otherwise this is the framework eating itself. **Note:** distinct from the "MC-as-product boundary" sprint task in Now: Framework Boundary & Identity — that's about creating a *private workspace* to hold maintainer product-thinking outside canonical, this is about *spec-ing the framework itself* as a product. Boundary first; deep dogfooding much later.

**[parked] Observability + UX polish.** `agent-dashboard.js` as a real browser UI (currently CLI), skill usage counter for pruning, `/mc:tour` v2 interactive walkthrough, `USER_GUIDE.md` split into tutorial + reference. *Trigger to pull forward:* maintainer hits real friction with current observability, OR onboards a second user.

**[parked] Token usage optimization.** Per-agent token tracking, per-provider cost dashboard, prompt compression for cross-provider, prompt cache for system-identity portion, tiered fallback `gpt-5.4 → mini → claude`, per-agent model env-var override. *Trigger to pull forward:* monthly provider spend exceeds a threshold the maintainer cares about, OR cost-sensitive consumer asks for it.

**[discussed] First-class fast "sprint" mode + Alex system redesign for parallelism.** Operator wants a dedicated build MODE — alongside solo / adhoc / oneshot — that is *fast by default* and does *more work in parallel*. The motivation is lived friction: today the fast path is assembled from separate levers — `/session:turbo --speed`, the `turbo` autonomy preset that is still **pending operator approval** (`paths.sprintFullAutonomy`), the multi-sprint parallel-lanes design (ADR-0002), and engine-sprint fast-close (RI-001) — while `/sprint:full` in adhoc mode still serializes through 4 Beta phase-boundary halts + a `tickets_pending` halt **per sprint**, and build dispatch is one-builder-at-a-time. A real "sprint mode" would: (1) make parallel builder fan-out the default for disjoint tickets/lanes (not a manual choice); (2) collapse the per-boundary Beta cadence into ONE batched upfront plan-consult with halts only on genuine ESCALATE / the gauntlet verdict (the batched-Beta pattern, made first-class); (3) bake in the smart gauntlet-necessity classifier (see Next: Skill Reliability) so low-risk lanes skip the gauntlet automatically and enforcer-class lanes keep it; (4) wire the bounded-dispatch / chunk-on-reap protection (RI-004) so parallel background builders don't silent-die. The broader ask is an **Alex system redesign** around this: rethink the α/β/γ/δ topology and the orchestrator loop so parallelism + speed are structural, not bolted on. **Scope guard:** speed must never lift the CLAUDE.md hard ceilings (push, paid signup, prod deploy) or the fail-safe-to-full-gauntlet rule. *Trigger to pull forward:* operator schedules the redesign sprint (high interest expressed 2026-06-02); a natural first concrete step is approving + wiring the existing `turbo` autonomy preset, then promoting batched-Beta + parallel-default into a named mode. Design RFC before implementation. *(DISCUSSED-2026-06-02.)*

**[parked] Multi-provider credential-shape registry for the custody enforcers.** *(operator ruling 2026-08-18: "leave for now… may be a multi-provider MC thing — come back after launch")* The vlad engine custody lane's fix-5 durability test (S-VLADW1-01, 2026-08-18) found four SDK credential env vars ABSENT from `SECRET_SHAPES` / `ENV_DENYLIST` — `AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK`, `ANTHROPIC_FOUNDRY_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_FOUNDRY_AUTH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_AWS_API_KEY` — disclosed as a live gap (the test goes RED if the list drifts), deliberately NOT silently patched. The generalization: one MC-side multi-provider secret-shape registry (Anthropic direct/Bedrock/Foundry/Vertex + OpenAI + Gemini credential env names and value shapes) consumed by the custody scanners and the spawn-shim denylist, so a product's custody control doesn't hand-maintain a per-provider list. *Trigger to pull forward:* Vlad W1 launch shipped, OR a second product adopts the custody lane, OR the durability test goes RED on a new provider var. Until then the four vars stay disclosed-open in the vlad tracker.

**[discussed] App navigation planning + persistent UI/UX knowledge system.** *(operator ask 2026-06-09)* As MC builds product features, navigation reliably DEGRADES — it gets cluttered (poor UX), or a feature gets built but never wired into a menu (unreachable). The fix is a navigation SYSTEM in the product-building pipeline, two halves: **(1) a persistent UI/UX knowledge layer** — a per-product, always-current map of the app's screens / menus / routes / information-architecture (sibling to the `_knowledge/` brain) that INFORMS the spec + design agents and is UPDATED on every feature change (so the agents always reason against the real current UI, not a stale assumption); **(2) an adaptive navigation planner** the spec/design agents consult — *before* adding or changing any feature, ALWAYS read the current nav, place the feature in the right spot, ensure every feature is reachable, and — when warranted — totally REDO the navigation (adaptive, not append-only), optimized for the app's JTBDs. Scope: ALL menus / nav structures; the standing invariant "no feature is unreachable AND every feature is in the right place." Pairs with the design-quality + product/design agent layer + the `agents:init` per-project tooling + the `_knowledge/` brain pattern. Design Qs: where the per-product nav-knowledge lives + its update trigger (any spec/build touching a route), how the planner decides append-vs-restructure, and the JTBD source it optimizes against (canon / audience dossier). *Trigger to pull forward:* a product hits nav-clutter or unreachable-feature friction, or the design-agent layer matures enough to own it. *(DISCUSSED-2026-06-09)*

## ✅ Shipped in v0.1.3 (2026-05-02)

- Product-side `/mc:release` driver
  (`scripts/mc/release-canonical.js`).
- `framework-manifest-guard.js` false-positive fix: runs
  `generate-framework-manifest.js --check` before blocking; allows
  multi-stage commits where manifest at HEAD already covers staged
  content.
- `scripts/paths/gate.js` now skips `.mc/transactions/` (transaction
  records are append-only event logs, not framework code).

## ✅ Shipped in v0.1.2 (2026-05-01)

Patch release closing the architecture-drift loop:

- Installer derives `.claude/paths.json` and per-project mc version from
  `framework/paths.registry.json` + `version.json` instead of hardcoding.
- New `framework/hooks.registry.json` is the single source of truth for hooks.
  `scripts/hooks/build.js` derives `.claude/settings.json` (hooks block) and
  `scripts/hooks/hook-manifest.json` from the registry. `warp-setup.js` and
  `scripts/hooks/test.js` consume the registry.
- `/mc:update` rewritten: cross-repo aware (`--source`/`--target`),
  robust source-tree-root walk, real migration runner (via
  `migrations-loader.applyAll`), real post-update check execution,
  transaction record + per-file backup, `MERGE_SAFE` no longer pretends a
  merge (customized files are now `MERGE_CONFLICT`).
- `/warp:promote` no longer hardcodes a source-repo name; detects it from
  `manifest.json#project.slug` or `package.json#name`.
- `path-lint.js` extension coverage extended to `ts/tsx/sh/ps1/yml/yaml`
  (was md/js/json only). New `path-literal-allowed` per-line escape.
- `spec-test-staleness.js` reads `paths.specsRoot` (was hardcoded
  <!-- path-literal-allowed: roadmap naming the deprecated literal -->
  `_requirements/04-features` — silent no-op since the rename).
- `release-gates.js` reference-integrity gate marked `manual` (was lying as
  `green`).
- README + USER_GUIDE list `/mc:update` as the primary inbound command;
  `/mc:sync` documented as deprecated alias.

## ✅ Shipped in v0.1.1 (2026-04-18)

The install-hardening batch. Every item below was a ROADMAP entry from 2026-04-17 or 04-18 that now ships in production.

### Installer foundation
- [x] **Ship-manifest system** — `.claude/framework-manifest.json` declares every shippable asset; installer iterates the manifest instead of hand-coded `copyDir` calls. Generator: `scripts/generate-framework-manifest.js`. (205 assets + 9 generated.)
- [x] **Framework-installed snapshot** — target projects get `.claude/framework-installed.json` at install; uninstall walks it exhaustively; re-install diffs old vs new for ghost-file detection.
- [x] **Ghost-file cleanup on re-install** — installer detects files declared by prior install but removed/renamed upstream; `--clean-ghosts` flag removes them.
- [x] **`--dry-run` actually works** — flag was parsed but unused; now prints the full plan (per-kind counts, would-skip existing, would-generate, ghost count) and exits without writes.
- [x] **Installer copy-scope gap closed** — first-install on aiweb missed 46 files (requirements + patterns + maps + top-level scripts). Manifest makes this impossible: if it's in the manifest, the installer sees it.
- [x] **Top-level scripts ship too** — `path-lint.js`, `dispatch-agent.js`, `generate-maps.js`, `generate-framework-manifest.js`.

### Installer UX
- [x] **CLAUDE.md auto-merge** — if target has existing `CLAUDE.md`, installer appends Alex identity with `---` separator; backup kept.
- [x] **AGENTS.md auto-merge** — same pattern; prior behavior silently kept user's AGENTS.md without Alex system, breaking γ dispatch.
- [x] **Restart banner handles both paths** — "already have Claude Code open? close + reopen. not open yet? just open" — replaces the old "YOU MUST RESTART NOW" that confused first-time users.
- [x] **`/warp:init` → `/mc:setup`** — resumable state-machine skill; 4 signals checked, only missing steps run. Safe to re-run. `/mc:uninstall` shipped.
- [x] **Pre-install backup** — `.mc-backup/<ts>/` captures CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, .gitignore, .claude/, scripts/hooks/ before any write.

### Hook correctness
- [x] **Hook schema: `type:"command"` required** — installer was writing just `{command}`; Claude Code's validator rejected at launch. Fixed via `cmd()` helper.
- [x] **Single-event keys** — `"Stop|SessionEnd|StopFailure"` pipe-joined was "Unknown hook event"; split into three top-level keys.
- [x] **Per-matcher hook merge** — if user has any pre-existing hook in an event, old logic skipped MC's whole set. Now: append MC hooks into matching matcher, dedup by command string. User's hooks preserved.
- [x] **merge-guard catches `+refspec` force-push** — was only catching `--force` and `-f`; `git push origin +main` bypassed the guard. Fixed.
- [x] **Framework-manifest guard** — PreToolUse Bash hook blocks commits that stage tracked assets without re-staging the manifest. Enforces "regenerate before commit." β DECIDE: block, don't mutate.

### Skills + docs
- [x] **`/discover:systems`** — 6-angle discovery (declarative/structural/behavioral/refgraph/convention/historical).
- [x] **`/mc:uninstall`** — clean removal with restore from `.mc-backup/`; consumes `framework-installed.json` for exhaustive file list.
- [x] **Attestation events schema** — `cat: "attestation"` in events.jsonl tracks learning → enforcement provenance. One-shot emitters: `scripts/tools/emit-attestation-events.js`, `emit-integrate-events.js`.
- [x] **USER_GUIDE §2 clarity** — modes are project-wide and persistent; adhoc still probes β with just α + user; oneshot is end-to-end-rebuild from requirements.
- [x] **USER_GUIDE §5.6 preflight ELI5** — 7-pass breakdown; ONLY for oneshot.
- [x] **`/sleep:deep` Phase 4 painting MANDATORY** — several cycles had skipped the ASCII art step; now self-check-gated.

### Privacy + public release
- [x] **Repo transitioned private → public** — `cygaco/MC` now public.
- [x] **History scrub via git-filter-repo** — 68 commits rewritten; zero references to private product/repo names in any commit.
- [x] **Redteam audit (4 parallel scans)** — 0 credentials, 0 PII, 0 tracked-but-ignored. IP hygiene scrub landed in 20+ files.
- [x] **smart-context Haiku timeout + payload caps** — was 8000ms on unbounded context; now 15000ms + per-source caps (60 learnings, 20 traces, 20 decisions).
